import time
import numpy as np

def _optimal_constant_length():
    target = 2535
    try:
        seq = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
        if isinstance(seq, (list, tuple, np.ndarray)):
            n = int(len(seq))
            if 1 <= n <= 20000:
                return n
    except Exception:
        pass
    return target

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Return an optimal non-negative sequence for evaluate_sequence.
    ...
    """
    n = _optimal_constant_length()
    seq = [1.0] * n
    ev = globals().get("evaluate_sequence")
    if callable(ev):
        try:
             score = float(ev(seq))
             print(f"Optimal constant sequence length {n}: evaluate_sequence = {score!r}")
             # If not finite, fallback to smaller
             if not np.isfinite(score):
                 seq = [1.0] * 2535
                 score = float(ev(seq))
                 print(...)
        except Exception as e:
             print("evaluate_sequence check failed:", e)
    else:
         print("evaluate_sequence not available; returning analytical optimum.")
    # Maybe if time available? no
    return [float(v) for v in seq]
```

def _select_optimal_length(preferred=2535, max_len=2535):
    try:
       parent = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
       if parent is not None:
           ln = int(len(parent))
           if ln > 0:
               # Use a moderate length. If parent is reasonably small, keep it.
               if ln <= max_len:
                   return ln
    except Exception:
       pass
    return preferred

Let's think about proving lower bound in code comments: At lag n-1, convolution equals sum a_i^2. Cauchy-Schwarz: (sum a_i)^2 <= n sum a_i^2. Since max_b >= sum a_i^2, objective >=2. Equality iff all a_i equal. Since evaluate_sequence clips to nonnegative, proof applies to clipped list. Returning constant achieves equality. This is strong.

The final code should not use closures/lambdas. We can define helper `_constant_sequence(n)`. It returns list. `_safe_score(seq)` maybe. Use top-level.

Let's write final code:
```
import numpy as np

def _constant_sequence(n=2535):
    """Return a constant nonnegative sequence of length n."""
    return [1.0] * int(n)

def _finite_score(seq):
    ev = globals().get("evaluate_sequence")
    if not callable(ev):
        return None
    try:
        return float(ev(seq))
    except Exception:
        return None

def _select_length():
    # Preferred length from previous construction, but avoid huge O(n^2) eval.
    preferred = 2535
    try:
        parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
        if parent is not None:
            n = int(len(parent))
            if 1 <= n <= 3000:
                return n
    except Exception:
        pass
    return preferred

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Return an analytically optimal sequence for the given evaluator.

    The evaluator clips all entries to nonnegative values. For any such
    nonnegative array a of length n, full autocorrelation at lag n-1 equals
    sum_i a_i^2. Cauchy-Schwarz gives sum_i a_i^2 >= (sum_i a_i)^2 / n, and
    therefore max autocorrelation >= (sum a)^2 / n. This implies
    evaluate_sequence(a) >= 2. Equality is attained exactly by a constant
    nonnegative vector. Hence no random/local search can beat 2; we return a
    constant vector of the previous construction's size (or 2535).
    """
    n = _select_length()
    seq = _constant_sequence(n)
    score = _finite_score(seq)
    if score is None:
        # evaluator unavailable; analytical optimum still returned.
        print("evaluate_sequence unavailable; returning analytical optimum length", n)
    elif np.isfinite(score):
        print(f"constant/{n} score={score!r}")
    else:
        print("unexpected nonfinite score for constant sequence; using fallback length 2535")
        seq = _constant_sequence(2535)
        score = _finite_score(seq)
        if score is not None and np.isfinite(score):
            print(f"fallback constant/{2535} score={score!r}")
    return [float(v) for v in seq]
```

```
import math

def _constant_nonnegative_sequence(n):
    """Return a list of positive floats of length n."""
    n = int(n)
    if n < 1:
        n = 2535
    return [1.0] * n

def _previous_length_if_safe(limit=3000):
    try:
        parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
        if parent is not None:
            n = int(len(parent))
            if 1 <= n <= limit:
                return n
    except Exception:
        pass
    return 2535

def _try_score(sequence):
    ev = globals().get("evaluate_sequence")
    if not callable(ev):
        return None
    try:
        return float(ev(sequence))
    except Exception:
        return None

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """..."""
    n = _previous_length_if_safe(3000)
    best = _constant_nonnegative_sequence(n)
    best_score = _try_score(best)
    if not (best_score is not None and math.isfinite(best_score)):
        fallback_n = 2535
        best = _constant_nonnegative_sequence(fallback_n)
        best_score = _try_score(best)
    if best_score is not None:
        print(f"Analytical optimum constant length={len(best)} score={best_score!r}")
    else:
        print("Analytical optimum constant sequence returned; evaluator unavailable.")
    return [float(x) for x in best]
```

   if sc < best_score*(1-1e-12): update, stagnation=0, maybe reduce sigma?

   iterations+=1
```

Detailed helper implementations:

`_central_sequence(n)`:
```
a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
a[0]=1.0
for i in range(n-1):
    a[i+1]=a[i]*(2*i+1)/(2*i+2)
s=a.sum()
if s>0: a/=s
return a
```
Check recurrence: i=0 -> a1=1*(1)/(2)=0.5. i=1 -> a2=0.5*(3)/(4)=0.375. Good.

`_score_array(a)`:
```
a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
s=a.sum()
if s < 0.01/n? evaluator after clipping requires s>=0.01; for scaled seq sum 1. OK.
b=np.convolve(a,a)
mb=b.max()
return float(2*a.size*mb/(s*s))
```
For search, if s=0 inf.
Use `n=a.size`.

`_normalize(a)`:
```
a=np.asarray(a,float)
a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
s=a.sum()
if not finite or s<=0: return np.ones(n)/n
return a/s
```
But if a has huge, clip; okay.

Random helpers top-level:
`_random_block_move(a, rng, sigma)`:
```
n=a.size
# choose length from candidates
L = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//8)))? We want up to n/4. Use L = min(n, int(np.exp(rng.normal(np.log(max(2,n//100)), 0.8)))) maybe. Simpler: L = int(rng.integers(1, n//4+1))
start=int(rng.integers(0, n-L))
mult=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=L))
u=a.copy()
u[start:start+L]=np.clip(u[start:start+L]*mult,0,1000)
return _normalize(u)
```
Need no numpy exp for each? okay.

`_gradient_step(a, eta, delta, rng)`:
```
n=a.size
b=np.convolve(a,a)
B=b.max()
T=(1.0-delta)*B
w=b-T
mask=w<0; w[mask]=0
if w.sum()<=0: return a
# convolve w with reversed a
g=np.convolve(w, a[::-1], mode='full')
g=g[n-1:2*n-1]
u=a*g
scale=np.mean(u)
if not finite or scale<=0: return a
z=u/scale
np.clip(z,0,20,z) # does np.clip with out? use z=np.minimum(z,20)
step=eta*z
u=a*np.exp(-step)
return _normalize(u)
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    import numpy as np, time, math? imports at top.
    n = 2535
    # maybe if height_sequence_1 has safe length use it? Use n = 2535 fixed.
    best = _central_sequence(n)
    best_score = _score_array(best)
    # candidate inits
    try:
       for cand in _initial_candidates(n):
          score=_score_array(cand)
          if finite and score<best_score: best=cand; best_score=score
       print(...)
       deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget_s)-30.0)
       rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
       sigma=0.08
       stagnation=0
       iter=0
       while time.monotonic() < deadline:
           if stagnation > 3000:
               sigma = min(0.6, sigma*1.2)
               stagnation=0
           # occasional gradient
           if iter % 200 == 0:
               eta = 0.05 + 0.2*float(rng.random())
               delta = 0.01 + 0.08*float(rng.random())
               cand = _gradient_step(best, eta, delta, rng)
           else:
               rng_method = rng.random()
               if rng_method < 0.7:
                   cand = _random_block_move(best, rng, sigma)
               elif rng_method < 0.9:
                   scale = float(np.mean(best))
                   cand = _random_transfer_move(best, rng, scale)
               else:
                   # smooth global tilt
                   cand = _global_tilt_move(best, rng)
           score = _score_array(cand)
           if finite and score < best_score:
               best = cand; best_score=score; stagnation=0; if score < 1.6 maybe? no
               print(iter, score)
           else:
               stagnation +=1
           iter+=1
    except Exception:
       # fallback
    # actual validation
    ev = globals().get('evaluate_sequence')
    if callable(ev):
       actual = None
       try: actual=float(ev(best.tolist()))
       except Exception: actual=None
       if actual is None or not math.isfinite(actual):
           best=_central_sequence(n)
           try: actual=float(ev(best.tolist()))
           except Exception: actual=float('inf')
       print('final', actual)
    return best.tolist()

Helper `_random_block_move`:
```
def _random_block_move(a, rng, sigma):
    n=int(a.size)
    maxL=max(1,n//4)
    L=int(max(1, min(maxL, rng.randint(1, maxL+1))))
    start=int(rng.randint(0, n-L))
    mult=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=L))
    u=a.copy()
    u[start:start+L]=np.clip(u[start:start+L]*mult, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return _normalize(u)
```
`rng.randint(1, maxL+1)` inclusive low exclusive high. Good.

`_random_transfer_move`:
```
def _random_transfer_move(a, rng, std):
    n=int(a.size)
    u=a.copy()
    i=int(rng.randint(0,n)); j=int(rng.randint(0,n))
    d=float(rng.normal(0.0, std))
    u[i]=max(0.0, u[i]+d)
    u[j]=max(0.0, u[j]-d)
    return _normalize(u)
```

`_global_tilt_move`:
```
def _global_tilt_move(a, rng):
    n=int(a.size)
    if n <= 2: return a.copy()
    x=np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)/(n-1)
    m=int(2 + rng.randint(0,4))
    base=np.ones(n,dtype=np.float64)
    for r in range(m):
        coef=float(rng.normal(0.0,0.08))
        base *= np.exp(coef * np.cos(np.pi*(r+1)*x))
    u=np.clip(a*base,0,1000)
    return _normalize(u)
```
No loop issues. Coefficients small; factors positive.

`_gradient_step`:
```
def _gradient_step(a, eta, delta):
    n=int(a.size)
    b=np.convolve(a,a)
    B=float(b.max())
    if not math.isfinite(B) or B <= 0: return a.copy()
    T=(1.0-delta)*B
    w=b-T
    np.maximum(w,0.0,out=w)
    if float(w.sum()) <=0: return a.copy()
    g=np.convolve(w, a[::-1], mode='full')
    g=g[n-1:2*n-1]
    u=a*g
    scale=float(np.mean(u))
    if not math.isfinite(scale) or scale <=0: return a.copy()
    z=u/scale
    z=np.clip(z,0.0,25.0)
    u=a*np.exp(-float(eta)*float(z))
    return _normalize(u)
```
Use `math.isfinite`. Good.

`_initial_candidates`:
Return list. Need avoid recomputed central repeated. Use local variable central.
```
def _initial_candidates(n):
    c=[]
    central=_central_sequence(n)
    c.append(central)
    for p in (0.45,0.5,0.55): c.append(_power_sequence(n,p))
    t_x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)/(max(1,n-1))
    for alpha in (0.3,0.7,1.5,3.0):
       a=np.exp(-alpha*t_x); c.append(_normalize(a)); c.append(_normalize(a[::-1].copy()))
    for eps in (0.2,0.5,0.8):
       a=1.0+eps*np.cos(np.pi*t_x); c.append(_normalize(np.clip(a,1e-12,1000)))
    for alpha in (0.25,0.8,2.0):
       a=central*np.exp(-alpha*t_x); c.append(_normalize(a))
       a=central*np.exp(-alpha*(1.0-t_x)); c.append(_normalize(a))
    return c
```
This is ~1+3+8+3+6=21 evals.

```python
"""
Search/proposal algorithm.

The objective is
    E(a) = 2*n*max_k conv(a,a)[k] / sum(a)^2
for nonnegative sequences a of length n.

A very strong nonnegative construction is the prefix of the Maclaurin
coefficients of (1-z)^(-1/2), i.e.
    a_i = binom(2i,i) / 4^i.
For this sequence the first n convolution coefficients are all exactly 1,
and the score approaches pi/2 from above as n grows.  This is much better
than the uniform sequence, whose score is exactly 2.

This program returns that construction, tries a few related parametric
initializations, and then runs a bounded local stochastic + subgradient
search to see if a finite-length refinement is possible.  The final returned
sequence is validated with the provided evaluate_sequence() when available.
"""

import math
import time
import numpy as np


def _normalize(a):
    """Clip to the evaluator's safe range and normalize the sum to one."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / a.size
    a = a / s
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return a


def _score_array(a):
    """Fast internal copy of the evaluator equation, on a numpy array."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-300:
        return float("inf")
    if s < 0.01:
        # The provided evaluator would return inf; keep internal behavior sane.
        return float("inf")
    b = np.convolve(a, a)
    mb = float(np.max(b))
    if not math.isfinite(mb):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * mb / (s * s))


def _actual_score(seq):
    """Safely call the external evaluator, if present, treating non-finite as inf."""
    ev = globals().get("evaluate_sequence")
    if not callable(ev):
        return float("inf")
    try:
        val = float(ev(seq))
        return val if math.isfinite(val) else float("inf")
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def _central_sequence(n):
    """
    Coefficients of (1-z)^(-1/2), normalized:
        c_0 = 1
        c_{i+1} = c_i * (2i+1) / (2i+2)
    """
    n = int(max(1, n))
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(n - 1):
        a[i + 1] = a[i] * float(2 * i + 1) / float(2 * i + 2)
    return _normalize(a)


def _power_sequence(n, p):
    """a_i proportional to (i+1)^(-p)."""
    n = int(max(1, n))
    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.power(idx, -float(p))
    return _normalize(a)


def _exp_sequence(n, alpha):
    """Exponential tilt a_i proportional to exp(-alpha*i/(n-1))."""
    n = int(max(1, n))
    if n == 1:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n - 1)
    return _normalize(np.exp(-float(alpha) * x))


def _cosine_sequence(n, eps):
    """Nonnegative cosine tilt 1 + eps*cos(pi*i/(n-1))."""
    n = int(max(1, n))
    if n == 1:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n - 1)
    a = 1.0 + float(eps) * np.cos(math.pi * x)
    a = np.clip(a, 1e-12, 1000.0)
    return _normalize(a)


def _initial_candidates(n):
    """A small set of diverse nonnegative starting points."""
    cands = []
    base = _central_sequence(n)
    cands.append(base)

    # Pure powers.
    for p in (0.45, 0.50, 0.55):
        cands.append(_power_sequence(n, p))

    # Exponential tilts and their reverses.
    for alpha in (0.3, 0.7, 1.5, 3.0):
        a = _exp_sequence(n, alpha)
        cands.append(a)
        cands.append(_normalize(a[::-1].copy()))

    # Cosine tilts.
    for eps in (0.2, 0.5, 0.8):
        cands.append(_cosine_sequence(n, eps))

    # Tilted central-binomial shapes.
    if n > 1:
        x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n - 1)
        for alpha in (0.25, 0.8, 2.0):
            cands.append(_normalize(base * np.exp(-float(alpha) * x)))
            cands.append(_normalize(base * np.exp(-float(alpha) * (1.0 - x))))

    return cands


def _random_block_move(a, rng, sigma):
    """Multiplicative perturbation of a random block, then renormalize."""
    n = int(a.size)
    maxL = max(1, n // 4)
    L = int(max(1, min(maxL, rng.randint(1, maxL + 1))))
    start = int(rng.randint(0, max(1, n - L)))
    mult = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), size=L))
    u = a.copy()
    u[start:start + L] = np.clip(u[start:start + L] * mult, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return _normalize(u)


def _random_transfer_move(a, rng, std):
    """Move a small amount of mass between two coordinates."""
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 1:
        return a.copy()
    u = a.copy()
    i = int(rng.randint(0, n))
    j = int(rng.randint(0, n))
    d = float(rng.normal(0.0, max(1e-12, float(std))))
    u[i] = max(0.0, u[i] + d)
    u[j] = max(0.0, u[j] - d)
    return _normalize(u)


def _global_tilt_move(a, rng):
    """Multiplied by a product of a few low-frequency cosine modulations."""
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 2:
        return a.copy()
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n - 1)
    m = int(2 + rng.randint(0, 4))
    factor = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for r in range(m):
        coef = float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.08))
        factor *= np.exp(coef * np.cos(math.pi * float(r + 1) * x))
    u = np.clip(a * factor, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return _normalize(u)


def _gradient_step(a, eta, delta):
    """
    Subgradient-inspired multiplicative step:
    put weight on convolution coefficients near the current maximum and
    multiplicatively reduce entries participating in those coefficients.
    """
    n = int(a.size)
    b = np.convolve(a, a)
    B = float(np.max(b))
    if not math.isfinite(B) or B <= 0.0:
        return a.copy()

    T = (1.0 - float(delta)) * B
    w = b - T
    np.maximum(w, 0.0, out=w)
    if float(np.sum(w)) <= 0.0:
        return a.copy()

    # g_j = sum_k w_k a_{k-j}, using convolution with reversed a.
    g = np.convolve(w, a[::-1], mode="full")
    g = g[n - 1:2 * n - 1]

    u = a * g
    scale = float(np.mean(u))
    if not math.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 0.0:
        return a.copy()

    z = u / scale
    z = np.clip(z, 0.0, 25.0)
    v = a * np.exp(-float(eta) * z)
    return _normalize(v)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Return a best-known nonnegative coefficient sequence.

    Uses the analytically motivated central-binomial construction as the
    main fallback, evaluates several related inits, and then spends the
    available time on a bounded random/subgradient local search.
    """
    n = 2535
    rng = np.random.RandomState(int(seed) % (2 ** 31 - 1))

    # Analytical-main fallback.
    base = _central_sequence(n)
    best = base.copy()
    best_score = _score_array(best)

    # Try simple diverse inits.
    try:
        for cand in _initial_candidates(n):
            sc = _score_array(cand)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best = cand
                best_score = sc
    except Exception:
        best = base.copy()
        best_score = _score_array(best)

    print("initial best score = {:.12f}".format(best_score))

    # Time-bounded local search.
    try:
        start_time = time.monotonic()
        margin = min(60.0, max(10.0, 0.02 * float(budget_s)))
        deadline = start_time + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - margin)

        sigma = 0.08
        stall = 0
        iterations = 0

        while time.monotonic() < deadline:
            if stall > 2000:
                sigma = min(0.55, sigma * 1.15)
                stall = 0

            # Mostly random local moves; occasionally a gradient-inspired move.
            if iterations % 700 == 0:
                eta = 0.04 + 0.16 * float(rng.rand())
                delta = 0.005 + 0.09 * float(rng.rand())
                cand = _gradient_step(best, eta, delta)
            else:
                mode = float(rng.rand())
                if mode < 0.72:
                    cand = _random_block_move(best, rng, sigma)
                elif mode < 0.9:
                    std = 6.0 * float(np.mean(best))
                    cand = _random_transfer_move(best, rng, std)
                else:
                    cand = _global_tilt_move(best, rng)

            sc = _score_array(cand)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best = cand
                best_score = sc
                stall = 0
                print("improved at iter {} score = {:.12f}".format(iterations, best_score))
            else:
                stall += 1

            iterations += 1
            if iterations % 20000 == 0:
                print("iter {} current internal score = {:.12f}".format(iterations, best_score))

        print("search finished: internal score = {:.12f}".format(best_score))
    except Exception:
        # If anything odd happens during search, keep the robust construction.
        best = base.copy()
        best_score = _score_array(best)
        print("search interrupted internally; using central-binomial fallback")

    # Final validation against the authoritative evaluator when available.
    candidate_lists = [base.tolist(), best.tolist()]
    best_final = base.tolist()
    best_final_score = _actual_score(best_final)

    for seq in candidate_lists[1:]:
        sc = _actual_score(seq)
        if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_final_score:
            best_final = seq
            best_final_score = sc

    # If actual evaluator was unavailable, fall back to internal scoring.
    if not math.isfinite(best_final_score):
        internal_scores = [
            (_score_array(np.asarray(best_final)), best_final),
            (_score_array(np.asarray(base.tolist())), base.tolist()),
        ]
        valid = [(s, seq) for s, seq in internal_scores if math.isfinite(s)]
        if valid:
            best_final_score, best_final = min(valid, key=lambda pair: pair[0])

    if math.isfinite(best_final_score):
        print("final score = {:.12f}".format(best_final_score))
    else:
        print("no finite final score was obtained; returning central-binomial sequence")

    # Ensure returned Python floats are plain floats.
    return [float(x) for x in best_final]
```
